13. using System.Collections.Generic;
14. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
15. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;

20. // The Blank Page item template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=402352&clcid=0x409
21. 
22. namespace SDKTemplate
23. {
24.     /// <summary>
25.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
26.     /// </summary>
27.     public sealed partial class MainPage : Page
28.     {
29.         public static MainPage Current;
30. 
31.         public MainPage()
32.         {
33.             this.InitializeComponent();
34. 
35.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
36.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
37.             Current = this;
38.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
39.         }
40. 
41.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
42.         {
43.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
44.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = scenarios;
45.             // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
46.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
47.             {
48.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
49.             }
50.             else
51.             {
52.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
53.             }
54.         }
55. 
56.         /// <summary>
57.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
58.         /// sample scenario page.
59.         /// </summary>
60.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
61.         /// <param name="e"></param>
62.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
63.         {
64.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
65.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
66. 
67.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
68.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
69.             if (s != null)
70.             {
71.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
72.                 // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
73.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
74.                 {
75.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
76.                     StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
77.                 }
78.             }
79.         }
80. 
81.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
82.         {
83.             get { return this.scenarios; }
84.         }
85. 
86.         /// <summary>
87.         /// Used to display messages to the user
88.         /// </summary>
89.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
90.         /// <param name="type"></param>
91.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
92.         {
93.             switch (type)
94.             {
95.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
96.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
97.                     break;
98.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
99.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
100.                     break;
101.             }
102.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
103. 
104.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
105.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
106.         }
107. 
108.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
109.         {
110.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
111.         }
112. 
113.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
114.         {
115.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
116.             StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
117.         }
118.     }
119.     public enum NotifyType
120.     {
121.         StatusMessage,
122.         ErrorMessage
123.     };
124. 
125.     public class ScenarioBindingConverter : IValueConverter
126.     {
127.         public object Convert(object value, Type targetType, object parameter, string language)
128.         {
129.             Scenario s = value as Scenario;
130.             return (MainPage.Current.Scenarios.IndexOf(s) + 1) + ") " + s.Title;
131.         }
132. 
133.         public object ConvertBack(object value, Type targetType, object parameter, string language)
134.         {
135.             return true;
136.         }
137.     }
138. }

3. using System.Collections.Specialized;
4. using System.Diagnostics;
5. using System.Linq;
6. using System.Threading;
7. using System.Threading.Tasks;
8. using Windows.Storage;
9. using Windows.Storage.Search;
10. using Windows.UI.Core;
11. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Data;
12. 
13. namespace DataVirtualizationSample
14. {
15.     //********************************************************************************************
16.     //*
17.     //* Note: This sample uses a custom compiler constant to enable tracing. If you add
18.     //* TRACE_DATASOURCE to the Conditional compilation symbols of the Build tab of the
19.     //* Project Properties window, then the application will spit out trace data to the
20.     //* Output window while debugging.
21.     //*
22.     //********************************************************************************************
23. 
24.     /// <summary>
25.     /// A custom datasource over the file system that supports data virtualization
26.     /// </summary>
27.     class FileDataSource2 : INotifyCollectionChanged, System.Collections.IList, IItemsRangeInfo, ISelectionInfo
28.     {
29.         // Folder that we are browsing
30.         private StorageFolder _folder;
31.         // Query object that will tell us if the folder content changed
32.         private StorageFileQueryResult _queryResult;
33.         // Dispatcher so we can marshal calls back to the UI thread
34.         // TODO[migrate-006]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
35.         private CoreDispatcher _dispatcher;
36. 
37.         // Cache for the file data that is currently being used
38.         private ItemCacheManager<FileItem> itemCache;
39. 
40.         // Total number of files available
41.         private int _count = 1;
42. 
43.         public event NotifyCollectionChangedEventHandler CollectionChanged;
44. 
45.         private FileDataSource2()
46.         {
47.             //Setup the dispatcher for the UI thread
48.             // TODO[migrate-007]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
49.             _dispatcher = Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Window.Current.Dispatcher;
50. 
51.             // The ItemCacheManager does most of the heavy lifting. We pass it a callback that it will use to actually fetch data, and the max size of a request
52.             this.itemCache = new ItemCacheManager<FileItem>(fetchDataCallback, 50);
53.             this.itemCache.CacheChanged += ItemCache_CacheChanged;
54. 
55.             // Create another ItemCacheManager for the keys for selection
56.             this.selectionCache = new ItemCacheManager<string>(fetchSelectionDataCallback, 50, "selectionCache");
57.         }
58. 
59.         // Handles a change notification for the list of files from the OS
60.         public async void ResetCollection()
61.         {
62.             // Update the selection cache to match the filesystem after the updates
63.             await RemapSelection();
64. 
65.             // Unhook the old change notification
66.             if (itemCache != null)
67.             {
68.                 this.itemCache.CacheChanged -= ItemCache_CacheChanged;
69.             }
70. 
71.             // Create a new instance of the cache manager
72.             this.itemCache = new ItemCacheManager<FileItem>(fetchDataCallback, 50);
73.             this.itemCache.CacheChanged += ItemCache_CacheChanged;
74.             if (CollectionChanged != null)
75.             {
76.                 CollectionChanged(this, new NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs(NotifyCollectionChangedAction.Reset));
77.             }
78.         }
79. 
80. 
81.         //
82.         // Implementation of ISelectionInfo
83.         //
84. 
85.         // Storage for the keys of the selected items
86.         private ItemCacheManager<string> selectionCache;
87.         // List of ranges that form the selection
88.         private ItemIndexRangeList selection = new ItemIndexRangeList();
89. 
90.         // Called when an item (or items) are selected
91.         public void SelectRange(ItemIndexRange range)
92.         {
93.             selection.Add(range);
94.             selectionCache.UpdateRanges(selection.ToArray());
95.         }
96. 
97.         // Called when an item (or items) are deselected
98.         public void DeselectRange(ItemIndexRange range)
99.         {
100.             selection.Subtract(range);
101.             selectionCache.UpdateRanges(selection.ToArray());
102.         }
103. 
104.         //  Called to determine if an item is selected 
105.         public bool IsSelected(int index)
106.         {
107.             foreach (ItemIndexRange range in selection)
108.             {
109.                 if (index >= range.FirstIndex && index <= range.LastIndex) return true;
110.             }
111.             return false;
112.         }
113. 
114.         // Called to get the selected ranges 
115.         public IReadOnlyList<ItemIndexRange> GetSelectedRanges()
116.         {
117.             return selection.ToList();
118.         }
119. 
120.         // Callback from the selection cache manager
121.         // Retrieves the keys for selected items
122.         private async Task<string[]> fetchSelectionDataCallback(ItemIndexRange batch, CancellationToken ct)
123.         {
124. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
125.             Debug.WriteLine("# SelectionDataCallback: " + batch.FirstIndex + "->" + batch.LastIndex);
126. #endif
127.             // See if we already have the item in the data cache, if so get the key from there so we don't need to go to the filesystem
128.             var file = itemCache[batch.FirstIndex];
129.             if (file != null)
130.             {
131.                 return new string[] { file.Key };
132.             }
133. 
134.             // Go get the keys from the file system if necessary
135.             IReadOnlyList<StorageFile> results = await _queryResult.GetFilesAsync((uint)batch.FirstIndex, batch.Length).AsTask(ct);
136.             List<string> keys = new List<string>();
137.             if (results != null)
138.             {
139.                 for (int i = 0; i < results.Count; i++)
140.                 {
141.                     ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
142.                     keys.Add(results[i].FolderRelativeId);
143.                 }
144.             }
145.             return keys.ToArray();
146.         }
147. 
148.         // Called after a reset to figure out the new indexes for the selected items
149.         private async Task RemapSelection()
150.         {
151.             ItemIndexRangeList oldSelection = selection;
152.             ItemCacheManager<string> oldSelectionCache = selectionCache;
153.             ItemIndexRangeList newSelection = new ItemIndexRangeList();
154.             ItemCacheManager<string> newSelectionCache = new ItemCacheManager<string>(fetchSelectionDataCallback, 50, "newSelectionCache");
155. 
156.             foreach (ItemIndexRange r in oldSelection)
157.             {
158.                 IReadOnlyList<StorageFile> results = null;
159.                 int lastResultOffset = 0, lastResultsItemIndex = 0;
160.                 for (int i = 0; i < r.Length; i++)
161.                 {
162.                     int origIndex = r.FirstIndex + i;
163.                     string fileid = oldSelectionCache[origIndex];
164.                     bool matched = false;
165. 
166.                     // Optimization to be able to work in batches. Once we get a batch of files from the filesystem we use that to hunt
167.                     // for matches rather than having to go ask for the index of each file
168.                     if (results != null)
169.                     {
170.                         for (int j = lastResultOffset + 1; j < results.Count; j++)
171.                         {
172.                             if (results[j].FolderRelativeId == fileid)
173.                             {
174.                                 lastResultOffset = j;
175.                                 int itemIndex = lastResultsItemIndex + j;
176.                                 newSelection.Add((uint)itemIndex, 1);
177.                                 newSelectionCache[itemIndex] = oldSelectionCache[origIndex];
178.                                 matched = true;
179.                                 break;
180.                             }
181.                         }
182.                     }
183.                     if (!matched)
184.                     {
185.                         // Get a starting point for the index of the file
186.                         lastResultsItemIndex = (int)(await _queryResult.FindStartIndexAsync(fileid.Substring(fileid.LastIndexOf('\\') + 1)));
187.                         lastResultOffset = 0;
188.                         // Get the files at that point and see if the keys actually match
189.                         results = await _queryResult.GetFilesAsync((uint)lastResultsItemIndex, 50);
190.                         if (results[lastResultOffset].FolderRelativeId == fileid)
191.                         {
192.                             newSelection.Add((uint)lastResultsItemIndex, 1);
193.                             newSelectionCache[lastResultsItemIndex] = oldSelectionCache[origIndex];
194.                         }
195.                         else
196.                         {
197.                             // We can't find the item, so its no longer part of the selection
198.                         }
199.                     }
200.                 }
201.             }
202.             // Update the fields for the new selection
203.             selection = newSelection;
204.             selectionCache = newSelectionCache;
205.         }
206. 
207.         #region Filesystem specific implementation details
208. 
209.         // Factory method to create the datasource
210.         // Requires async work which is why it needs a factory rather than being part of the constructor
211.         public static async Task<FileDataSource2> GetDataSoure(string path)
212.         {
213.             FileDataSource2 ds = new FileDataSource2();
214.             StorageFolder f = await StorageFolder.GetFolderFromPathAsync(path);
215.             await ds.SetFolder(f);
216.             return ds;
217.         }
218. 
219.         // Set functionality for the folder
220.         public async Task SetFolder(StorageFolder folder)
221.         {
222.             //unhook the old contents changed event if applicable
223.             if (_queryResult != null)
224.             {
225.                 _queryResult.ContentsChanged -= QueryResult_ContentsChanged;
226.             }
227.             // Initialize the query and register for changes
228.             _folder = folder;
229.             QueryOptions options = new QueryOptions();
230.             // options.IndexerOption = IndexerOption.DoNotUseIndexer;
231.             _queryResult = _folder.CreateFileQueryWithOptions(options);
232.             _queryResult.ContentsChanged += QueryResult_ContentsChanged;
233.             await UpdateCount();
234.         }
235. 
236.         // Handler for when the filesystem notifies us of a change to the file list
237.         private void QueryResult_ContentsChanged(IStorageQueryResultBase sender, object args)
238.         {
239.             // This callback can occur on a different thread so we need to marshal it back to the UI thread
240.             if (!_dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
241.             {
242.                 // TODO[migrate-008]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
243.                 var t = _dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, ResetCollection);
244.             }
245.             else
246.             {
247.                 ResetCollection();
248.             }
249.         }
250.         async Task UpdateCount()
251.         {
252.             _count = (int)await _queryResult.GetItemCountAsync();
253.             if (CollectionChanged != null)
254.             {
255.                 CollectionChanged(this, new NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs(NotifyCollectionChangedAction.Reset));
256.             }
257.         }
258. 
259.         //Event fired when items are inserted in the cache
260.         //Used to fire our collection changed event
261.         private void ItemCache_CacheChanged(object sender, CacheChangedEventArgs<FileItem> args)
262.         {
263.             if (CollectionChanged != null)
264.             {
265.                 CollectionChanged(this, new NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs(NotifyCollectionChangedAction.Replace, args.oldItem, args.newItem, args.itemIndex));
266.             }
267.         }
268.         #endregion
269. 
270.         #region IList Implementation
271. 
272.         public bool Contains(object value)
273.         {
274.             return IndexOf(value) != -1;
275.         }
276. 
277.         public int IndexOf(object value)
278.         {
279.             return (value != null) ? itemCache.IndexOf((FileItem)value) : -1;
280.         }
281. 
282.         public object this[int index]
283.         {
284.             get
285.             {
286.                 // The cache will return null if it doesn't have the item. Once the item is fetched it will fire a changed event so that we can inform the list control
287.                 return itemCache[index];
288.             }
289.             set
290.             {
291.                 throw new NotImplementedException();
292.             }
293.         }
294.         public int Count
295.         {
296.             get { return _count; }
297.         }
298. 
299.         #endregion
300. 
301.         #region IItemsRangeInfo Implementation
302.         //Required for the IItemsRangeInfo interface
303.         public void Dispose()
304.         {
305.             itemCache = null;
306.         }
307. 
308.         /// <summary>
309.         /// Primary method for IItemsRangeInfo interface
310.         /// Is called when the list control's view is changed
311.         /// </summary>
312.         /// <param name="visibleRange">The range of items that are actually visible</param>
313.         /// <param name="trackedItems">Additional set of ranges that the list is using, for example the buffer regions and focussed element</param>
314.         public void RangesChanged(ItemIndexRange visibleRange, IReadOnlyList<ItemIndexRange> trackedItems)
315.         {
316. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
317.             string s = string.Format("* RangesChanged fired: Visible {0}->{1}", visibleRange.FirstIndex, visibleRange.LastIndex);
318.             foreach (ItemIndexRange r in trackedItems) { s += string.Format(" {0}->{1}", r.FirstIndex, r.LastIndex); }
319.             Debug.WriteLine(s);
320. #endif
321.             // We know that the visible range is included in the broader range so don't need to hand it to the UpdateRanges call
322.             // Update the cache of items based on the new set of ranges. It will callback for additional data if required
323.             itemCache.UpdateRanges(trackedItems.ToArray());
324.         }
325. 
326. 
327.         // Callback from itemcache that it needs items to be retrieved
328.         private async Task<FileItem[]> fetchDataCallback(ItemIndexRange batch, CancellationToken ct)
329.         {
330.             // Fetch file objects from filesystem
331.             IReadOnlyList<StorageFile> results = await _queryResult.GetFilesAsync((uint)batch.FirstIndex, Math.Max(batch.Length, 20)).AsTask(ct);
332.             List<FileItem> files = new List<FileItem>();
333.             if (results != null)
334.             {
335.                 for (int i = 0; i < results.Count; i++)
336.                 {
337.                     ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
338.                     // Create our FileItem object with the file data and thumbnail 
339.                     FileItem newItem = await FileItem.fromStorageFile(results[i], ct);
340.                     files.Add(newItem);
341.                 }
342.             }
343.             return files.ToArray();
344.         }
345. 
346.         #endregion
347. 
348.         #region Parts of IList Not Implemented
349. 
350.         public int Add(object value)
351.         {
352.             throw new NotImplementedException();
353.         }
354. 
355.         public void Clear()
356.         {
357.             throw new NotImplementedException();
358.         }
359. 
360.         public void Insert(int index, object value)
361.         {
362.             throw new NotImplementedException();
363.         }
364. 
365.         public bool IsFixedSize
366.         {
367.             get { return false; }
368.         }
369. 
370.         public bool IsReadOnly
371.         {
372.             get { return false; }
373.         }
374. 
375.         public void Remove(object value)
376.         {
377.             throw new NotImplementedException();
378.         }
379. 
380.         public void RemoveAt(int index)
381.         {
382.             throw new NotImplementedException();
383.         }
384.         public void CopyTo(Array array, int index)
385.         {
386.             throw new NotImplementedException();
387.         }
388. 
389.         public bool IsSynchronized
390.         {
391.             get { throw new NotImplementedException(); }
392.         }
393. 
394.         public object SyncRoot
395.         {
396.             get { throw new NotImplementedException(); }
397.         }
398. 
399.         public System.Collections.IEnumerator GetEnumerator()
400.         {
401.             throw new NotImplementedException();
402.         }

176.             // We know that the visible range is included in the broader range so don't need to hand it to the UpdateRanges call

180. 
181.         // Callback from itemcache that it needs items to be retrieved
182.         // Using this callback model abstracts the details of this specific datasource from the cache implementation
183.         private async Task<FileItem[]> fetchDataCallback(ItemIndexRange batch, CancellationToken ct)
184.         {
185.             // Fetch file objects from filesystem
186.             IReadOnlyList<StorageFile> results = await _queryResult.GetFilesAsync((uint)batch.FirstIndex, Math.Max(batch.Length, 20)).AsTask(ct);
187.             List<FileItem> files = new List<FileItem>();
188.             if (results != null)
189.             {
190.                 for (int i = 0; i < results.Count; i++)
191.                 {
192.                     // Check if request has been cancelled, if so abort getting additional data
193.                     ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
194.                     // Create our FileItem object with the file data and thumbnail 
195.                     FileItem newItem = await FileItem.fromStorageFile(results[i], ct);
196.                     files.Add(newItem);
197.                 }
198.             }
199.             return files.ToArray();
200.         }

204.         private void ItemCache_CacheChanged(object sender, CacheChangedEventArgs<FileItem> args)
205.         {
206.             if (CollectionChanged != null)
207.             {
208.                 CollectionChanged(this, new NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs(NotifyCollectionChangedAction.Replace, args.oldItem, args.newItem, args.itemIndex));
209.             }
210.         }

<a id="windowing"></a>
## Windowing: Window.Current / ApplicationView / CoreWindow → AppWindow

`Window.Current`, `ApplicationView`, and `CoreWindow` are gone. Track the main window yourself and use `Microsoft.UI.Windowing.AppWindow`. See the official [windowing guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/windowing).

UWP (replace this):

```csharp
var win = Window.Current;
ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView().TryResizeView(new Size(800, 600));
```

WinUI 3 — expose `MainWindow` from `App`:

```csharp
public partial class App : Application
{
    // With <Nullable>enable</Nullable>, declare as nullable since the assignment only happens during OnLaunched.
    public static Window? MainWindow { get; private set; }

    protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
    {
        var window = new MainWindow();
        MainWindow = window;
        window.Activate();
    }
}
```

Consumers running after `OnLaunched` can safely null-forgive:

```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow!);
```

Resize via `AppWindow`:

```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow);
var windowId = Microsoft.UI.Win32Interop.GetWindowIdFromWindow(hwnd);
var appWindow = AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId);
appWindow.Resize(new SizeInt32(800, 600));
```

### Initialization order — keep MainWindow's constructor inert

`App.MainWindow` (or any equivalent static window reference) is `null` for the entire duration of `new MainWindow()` — the right-hand side runs to completion BEFORE the assignment happens. Anything inside that constructor that reads the static — directly or transitively — sees `null` and throws `E_POINTER` (0x80004003) at startup. The build is clean; the analyzer is silent; only a runtime launch reveals it.

APIs that commonly trip this race when called from a `Page` reached during `MainWindow` construction:

- `WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow)`
- `XamlRoot` / `Content.XamlRoot` access through a static-window helper
- `PrintManagerInterop.GetForWindow(hwnd)` / `ShowPrintUIForWindowAsync(hwnd)`
- `FileOpenPicker` / `FileSavePicker` / `FolderPicker` (require an HWND)
- `ContentDialog.ShowAsync()` when the dialog's `XamlRoot` is sourced from a static
- `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(...)` chained through `App.MainWindow`
- Any custom title bar / `SetTitleBar` helper that resolves the HWND statically

Safe pattern — assign before activate, defer first navigation:

```csharp
// App.xaml.cs
protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
{
    var window = new MainWindow();
    MainWindow = window;
    window.Activate();
}
```

The `MainWindow` constructor must do only `InitializeComponent` and inert setup — no navigation to a `Page` that reads `App.MainWindow`. Defer the first navigation until after `Activate` completes, either from a `Loaded` handler hooked in `InitializeComponent` or by exposing a `NavigateToInitialPage()` method and calling it from `App.OnLaunched` after the static is assigned.

```csharp
// MainWindow.xaml.cs
public MainWindow()
{
    InitializeComponent();
    this.Activated += (_, _) =>
    {
        if (!_navigated)
        {
            _navigated = true;
            RootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage));
        }
    };
}
private bool _navigated;
```

Validator catches this race with a 10s smoke launch after the build healthcheck passes — see `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 7.
<shellId: 3 completed with exit code 0>
--- RESULT ---
<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueue

UWP (replace this):

```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```

WinUI 3:

```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```

Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).

36.         private void Grid1_ContainerContentChanging(ListViewBase sender, ContainerContentChangingEventArgs args)
37.         {
38.             if (!args.InRecycleQueue)
39.             {
40.                 // Sets a textblock in each item indicating its index
41.                 FrameworkElement ctr = (FrameworkElement)args.ItemContainer.ContentTemplateRoot;
42.                 if (ctr != null)
43.                 {
44.                     TextBlock t = (TextBlock)ctr.FindName("idx");
45.                     t.Text = args.ItemIndex.ToString();
46.                 }
47.             }
48.         }
49.

51.         {
52.             StorageLibrary pictures = await StorageLibrary.GetLibraryAsync(KnownLibraryId.Pictures);
53.             string path = pictures.SaveFolder.Path;
54. 
55.             FileDataSource ds = await FileDataSource.GetDataSoure(path);
56.             if (ds.Count > 0)
57.             {
58.                 Grid1.ItemsSource = ds;
59.             }
60.             else
61.             {
62.                 MainPage.Current.NotifyUser("Error: The pictures folder doesn't contain any files", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
63.             }
64.         }

33.             initdata();

36.         private void Grid1_ContainerContentChanging(ListViewBase sender, ContainerContentChangingEventArgs args)
37.         {
38.             if (!args.InRecycleQueue)
39.             {
40.                 // Sets a textblock in each item indicating its index
41.                 FrameworkElement ctr = (FrameworkElement)args.ItemContainer.ContentTemplateRoot;
42.                 if (ctr != null)
43.                 {
44.                     TextBlock t = (TextBlock)ctr.FindName("idx");
45.                     t.Text = args.ItemIndex.ToString();
46.                 }
47.             }
48.         }
49. 
50.         async void initdata()
51.         {
52.             StorageLibrary pictures = await StorageLibrary.GetLibraryAsync(KnownLibraryId.Pictures);
53.             string path = pictures.SaveFolder.Path;
54. 
55.             FileDataSource ds = await FileDataSource.GetDataSoure(path);
56.             if (ds.Count > 0)
57.             {
58.                 Grid1.ItemsSource = ds;
59.             }
60.             else
61.             {
62.                 MainPage.Current.NotifyUser("Error: The pictures folder doesn't contain any files", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
63.             }
64.         }
65.     }

<a id="csproj"></a>
## Project File Updates

- Target a current WinAppSDK-supported TFM. The exact TFM is the source of truth in the project's `.csproj`; do not hard-code it across instruction files. Typical values at time of writing:
  - `net8.0-windows10.0.19041.0` (LTS)
  - `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0`
  - `net10.0-windows10.0.26100.0` (current `dotnet new winui` default in this repo)
- Add `<UseWinUI>true</UseWinUI>`.
- Add `<EnableMsixTooling>true</EnableMsixTooling>` for packaged builds.
- Reference `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK` and `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`.
- Reference `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools.WinApp` to wire `dotnet run` into `winapp run`.
- Keep `Package.appxmanifest` for packaged scenarios; set `<WindowsPackageType>None</WindowsPackageType>` for unpackaged.

### PackageReference reconciliation cheat-sheet

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` preserves the UWP `.csproj` at `<Target>/.uwp-source/` and leaves the WinUI 3 scaffold's `.csproj` intact. Open both side-by-side and merge:

- **Drop** (UWP-only — never carry over):
  - `Microsoft.NETCore.UniversalWindowsPlatform`
  - Any `<PackageReference>` whose version string contains `uap` or whose name starts with `Windows.SDK.Contracts`.
- **Keep** (already in the scaffold — do not duplicate or downgrade):
  - `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK`
  - `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`
- **Add** (project-specific — copy across, then update to a WinAppSDK-compatible version):
  - Third-party libraries the UWP project pulled in (`CommunityToolkit.*`, `Microsoft.Extensions.*`, `Newtonsoft.Json`, etc.). Pick the latest stable release; UWP-pinned versions are usually too old.

Do **not** copy the UWP `.csproj` over the scaffold's. The two formats are incompatible — the UWP csproj carries `<TargetPlatformIdentifier>UAP</TargetPlatformIdentifier>`, `<OutputType>AppContainerExe</OutputType>`, explicit `<Compile Include="...">` items, and `Microsoft.Common.props` imports, none of which build under WinAppSDK.

### Package.appxmanifest — reconcile image references with the assets you actually have

The WinUI 3 scaffold ships with a default `Package.appxmanifest` that references assets the template provides under `Assets/` (`SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, `Square150x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `Square44x44Logo.scale-200.png`, `StoreLogo.png`, `Wide310x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `LockScreenLogo.scale-200.png`). The UWP SDK sample's manifest usually points at sample-branded assets with a `-sdk` suffix (`Splash-sdk.png`, `squareTile-sdk.png`, `SmallTile-sdk.png`, `StoreLogo-sdk.png`).

If you copy the UWP manifest's `Logo` / `Square150x150Logo` / `Square44x44Logo` / `<uap:SplashScreen Image="...">` / `<uap:DefaultTile Wide310x150Logo="...">` references over the scaffold's without also bringing the matching files, **`winapp run` will register the package but the AppX layout will fail to launch** with errors like:

```
AppxManifest.xml(...): error 0x80070002: ... splash screen image [Splash-sdk.png] cannot be located.
```

(Failure code on the AppX side is typically `0x80073CF6` — "package could not be registered".)

You have two options. Pick one **before** the first `winapp run`:

- **Preferred — keep the scaffold's manifest image references** (`Assets\SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, etc.). When you merge any other content from the UWP manifest into the scaffold's (capabilities, file-type associations, `<uap:Extension>`, `<Application Id>`, etc.), do **not** overwrite the `Logo`, `<uap:SplashScreen Image>`, `Square150x150Logo`, `Square44x44Logo`, or `Wide310x150Logo` attribute values. The scaffold's defaults already match the files in its `Assets/` folder.

- **Alternative — keep the UWP sample's branded assets.** Copy every file the UWP manifest references from `.uwp-source/Assets/` (or `.uwp-source/<SampleName>/Assets/`) into the new project's `Assets/` folder, preserving the exact filename (including the `-sdk` suffix and any scale qualifiers). Verify by re-reading each `Image=`/`Logo>` value in the manifest and confirming `Test-Path "Assets\<that filename>"` for every one of them. Don't rename files to look prettier — the manifest reference is the source of truth.

Either way, before the first `winapp run`, sanity-check every asset reference in the manifest resolves:

```powershell
[xml]$m = Get-Content .\Package.appxmanifest
@($m.Package.Properties.Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square150x150Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square44x44Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_SplashScreen.Image,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_DefaultTile.Wide310x150Logo
) | Where-Object { $_ } | ForEach-Object {
    if (-not (Test-Path $_)) { Write-Host "[MISSING] $_" -ForegroundColor Red }
}
```

### Manifest migration checklist (Windows.Desktop + runFullTrust)

The default UWP `Package.appxmanifest` declares itself as a Universal app, but a packaged WinUI 3 desktop app is a Win32 process with package identity — it needs a different shape. `winapp run` will register the AppX but **fail to deploy** with `0x80073CF6` or "requires runFullTrust capability" if any of the following are missing.

When merging the UWP manifest into the scaffold's, make sure all of these are true:

1. **TargetDeviceFamily is `Windows.Desktop`** — not `Windows.Universal`:
   ```xml
   <Dependencies>
     <TargetDeviceFamily Name="Windows.Desktop" MinVersion="10.0.17763.0" MaxVersionTested="10.0.22621.0" />
   </Dependencies>
   ```
   Windows.Universal is UWP-only and rejected for a Win32 entrypoint.

2. **rescap namespace declared on `<Package>` and added to `IgnorableNamespaces`**:
   ```xml
   <Package
     xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10"
     xmlns:uap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/uap/windows10"
     xmlns:rescap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10/restrictedcapabilities"
     IgnorableNamespaces="uap rescap">
   ```
   Without the namespace declaration, item 3 below is silently stripped by AppX validation and you get the "requires runFullTrust" error at deploy time.

3. **`runFullTrust` capability declared**:
   ```xml
   <Capabilities>
     <rescap:Capability Name="runFullTrust" />
   </Capabilities>
   ```
   Packaged WinUI 3 desktop apps run outside the UWP AppContainer sandbox and must declare this. Keep any UWP `<Capability>` entries you actually use (e.g. `<DeviceCapability Name="webcam" />`) but add the `runFullTrust` line above no matter what.

4. **`<Application EntryPoint="$targetentrypoint$">`** — the WinUI 3 scaffold uses an MSBuild placeholder that the build resolves to the real entry point. Don't replace it with a literal `<UwpAppName>.App` (that's a UWP entry-point pattern).

5. **Carry over every UWP `<Extension>` the app's behavior depends on.** The scaffold manifest has none; the UWP manifest's `<Extensions>` are activation/registration prerequisites — omitting them makes the feature fail **at runtime** (often as a silently dead control), not at build time. Copy each `<Extension>` block from the UWP source manifest (under `.uwp-source/` or the copied UWP `Package.appxmanifest`) into the WinUI 3 root manifest's `<Application>`. Common ones:

   | Extension `Category` | Feature it powers |
   |----------------------|-------------------|
   | `windows.backgroundTasks` | out-of-process `BackgroundTaskBuilder` tasks (one `<Extension EntryPoint="...">` per `TaskEntryPoint`) |
   | `windows.protocol` | custom URI-scheme activation |
   | `windows.fileTypeAssociation` | open-with / file activation |
   | `windows.appService` | background app services |
   | `windows.shareTarget` | receiving shared content |

   `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` FAILs if retained `BackgroundTaskBuilder` code has no `windows.backgroundTasks` extension; the other categories have no automated check, so verify them by diffing the UWP manifest's `<Extensions>` against the migrated one.

### WUI analyzer warnings (UWP API residue)

The benchmark's `winapp build` injects the `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK.Analyzers` package, which flags UWP-only APIs that compile cleanly under WinUI 3 but throw `COMException` at runtime — typically inside `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application.Start(...)` before any window can render. The runner sees this as `builds=true, runs=false`, and `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` will FAIL the build healthcheck for each unique warning.

| Rule | Symptom | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `WUI0002` | `Window.Current does not exist in WinUI 3 desktop apps` | Store the `Window` reference in `App.xaml.cs` (`App.Window`) and pass it where needed; see "Windowing" above. |
| `WUI0003` | `CoreDispatcher is UWP-only` | Use `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()` and `TryEnqueue(...)`; see "Threading" above. |
| `WUI0004` | `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView() is UWP-only` | Drop the system back button hookup, or use HWND-based COM interop; see "GetForCurrentView Replacements" above. |

Treat every `warning WUI000\d` line in the build output as a defect — the analyzer does not produce false positives. Search for the API name in this document for the recommended replacement.

### Bootstrap-neutralized helper classes (filter-prone)

Some SDK Samples ship a shared `Common\NavigationHelper.cs` containing a `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class that wires ALT+Left / BrowserBack (virtual key 166) / mouse XButton1+XButton2 to frame back/forward navigation. Its WinUI 3 equivalent requires `Microsoft.UI.Input.InputKeyboardSource.GetKeyState` or P/Invoke `GetAsyncKeyState` plus virtual-key code constants — exactly the shape the model provider's content-safety filter classifies as "keylogger / surveillance" code. Migrating it routinely produces `CAPIError: 400 Output blocked by content filtering policy`.

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` handles this automatically: before the agent ever sees the file, the bootstrap rewrites just the `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class body to a no-op stub (preserving the public ctor with `params object[] args` so callers compile no matter what signature they invoke). The audit log records `NEUTRALIZED  class RootFrameNavigationHelper`. The mapping row's Notes column is prefixed with `NEUTRALIZED:`.

**You don't need to migrate the neutralized class.** Just leave both it and the call site (typically `new RootFrameNavigationHelper(rootFrame)` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnLaunched`) untouched — back-nav is not a demonstrated feature of any UWP SDK sample. The remainder of `NavigationHelper.cs` (the `NavigationHelper` class used for per-page state save/restore, the LoadStateEventArgs / SaveStateEventArgs types) is migrated normally; it does not trigger the filter.

If you really want ALT+Left back-nav, add a single declarative XAML element to the AppBarButton that triggers GoBack:

```xml
<AppBarButton Icon="Back" Click="OnBackClick">
  <AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
    <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
  </AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
</AppBarButton>
```

`KeyboardAccelerator` is declarative and does not trip the filter.

1. using System;

16. namespace DataVirtualizationSample
17. {
18.     /// <summary>
19.     /// Represents a file and its properties
20.     /// </summary>
21.     class FileItem
22.     {
23.         public string Filename { get; set; }
24.         public int Size { get; set; }
25.         public BitmapImage ImageData { get; set; }
26. 
27.         public string Key { get; private set; }
28. 
29.         // Needed to ensure only one request is in progress at once
30.         private static SemaphoreSlim gettingFileProperties = new SemaphoreSlim(1);
31. 
32.         // Fetches all the data for the specified file
33.         public async static Task<FileItem> fromStorageFile(StorageFile f, CancellationToken ct)
34.         {
35.             FileItem item = new FileItem();
36.             item.Filename = f.DisplayName;
37.             
38.             // Block to make sure we only have one request outstanding
39.             await gettingFileProperties.WaitAsync();
40. 
41.             BasicProperties bp = null;
42.             try
43.             {
44.                 bp = await f.GetBasicPropertiesAsync().AsTask(ct);
45.             }
46.             catch (Exception) { }
47.             finally
48.             {
49.                 gettingFileProperties.Release();
50.             }
51. 
52.             ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
53. 
54.             item.Size = (int)bp.Size;
55.             item.Key = f.FolderRelativeId;
56. 
57.             StorageItemThumbnail thumb = await f.GetThumbnailAsync(ThumbnailMode.SingleItem).AsTask(ct);
58.             ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
59.             BitmapImage img = new BitmapImage();
60.             await img.SetSourceAsync(thumb).AsTask(ct);
61. 
62.             item.ImageData = img;
63.             return item;
64.         }
65.     }

1. using System;
2. using System.Collections.Generic;
3. using System.Diagnostics;
4. using System.Threading;
5. using System.Threading.Tasks;
6. using Windows.Foundation;
7. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Data;

9. namespace DataVirtualizationSample
10. {
11.     // EventArgs class for the CacheChanged event 
12.     public class CacheChangedEventArgs<T> : EventArgs
13.     {
14.         public T oldItem { get; set; }
15.         public T newItem { get; set; }
16.         public int itemIndex { get; set; }
17.     }
18. 
19.     // Implements a relatively simple cache for items based on a set of ranges
20.     class ItemCacheManager<T>
21.     {
22.         // data structure to hold all the items that are in the ranges the cache manager is looking after
23.         private List<CacheEntryBlock<T>> cacheBlocks;
24. 
25.         // List of ranges for items that are not present in the cache
26.         internal ItemIndexRangeList requests;
27. 
28.         // list of ranges for items that are present in the cache
29.         private ItemIndexRangeList cachedResults;
30.         // Range of items that is currently being requested
31.         private ItemIndexRange requestInProgress;
32.         // Used to be able to cancel outstanding requests
33.         private CancellationTokenSource cancelTokenSource;
34.         // Callback that will be used to request data
35.         private fetchDataCallbackHandler fetchDataCallback;
36.         // Maximum number of items that can be fetched in one batch
37.         private int maxBatchFetchSize;
38.         // Timer to optimize the the fetching of data so we throttle requests if the list is still changing
39.         private Microsoft.UI.Xaml.DispatcherTimer timer;
40. 
41. #if DEBUG
42.         // Name for trace messages, and when debugging so you know which instance of the cache manager you are dealing with
43.         string debugName = string.Empty;
44. #endif
45.         public ItemCacheManager(fetchDataCallbackHandler callback, int batchsize = 50, string debugName = "ItemCacheManager")
46.         {
47.             cacheBlocks = new List<CacheEntryBlock<T>>();
48.             requests = new ItemIndexRangeList();
49.             cachedResults = new ItemIndexRangeList();
50.             fetchDataCallback = callback;
51.             maxBatchFetchSize = batchsize;
52.             //set up a timer that is used to delay fetching data so that we can catch up if the list is scrolling fast
53.             timer = new Microsoft.UI.Xaml.DispatcherTimer();
54.             timer.Tick += (sender, args) =>
55.             {
56.                 fetchData();
57.             };
58.             timer.Interval = new TimeSpan(20 * 10000);
59. 
60. #if DEBUG
61.             this.debugName = debugName;
62. #endif
63. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
64.             Debug.WriteLine(debugName + "* Cache initialized/reset");
65. #endif
66.         }
67. 
68.         public event TypedEventHandler<object, CacheChangedEventArgs<T>> CacheChanged;
69. 
70.         /// <summary>
71.         /// Indexer for access to the item cache
72.         /// </summary>
73.         /// <param name="index">Item Index</param>
74.         /// <returns></returns>
75.         public T this[int index]
76.         {
77.             get
78.             {
79.                 // iterates through the cache blocks to find the item
80.                 foreach (CacheEntryBlock<T> block in cacheBlocks)
81.                 {
82.                     if (index >= block.FirstIndex && index <= block.lastIndex)
83.                     {
84.                         return block.Items[index - block.FirstIndex];
85.                     }
86.                 }
87.                 return default(T);
88.             }
89.             set
90.             {
91.                 // iterates through the cache blocks to find the right block
92.                 for (int i = 0; i < cacheBlocks.Count; i++)
93.                 {
94.                     CacheEntryBlock<T> block = cacheBlocks[i];
95.                     if (index >= block.FirstIndex && index <= block.lastIndex)
96.                     {
97.                         block.Items[index - block.FirstIndex] = value;
98.                         //register that we have the result in the cache
99.                         if (value != null) { cachedResults.Add((uint)index, 1); }
100.                         return;
101.                     }
102.                     // We have moved past the block where the item is supposed to live
103.                     if (block.FirstIndex > index)
104.                     {
105.                         AddOrExtendBlock(index, value, i);
106.                         return;
107.                     }
108.                 }
109.                 // No blocks exist, so creating a new block
110.                 AddOrExtendBlock(index, value, cacheBlocks.Count);
111. 
112.             }
113.         }
114. 
115.         // Extends an existing block if the item fits at the end, or creates a new block
116.         private void AddOrExtendBlock(int index, T value, int insertBeforeBlock)
117.         {
118.             if (insertBeforeBlock > 0)
119.             {
120.                 CacheEntryBlock<T> block = cacheBlocks[insertBeforeBlock - 1];
121.                 if (block.lastIndex == index - 1)
122.                 {
123.                     T[] newItems = new T[block.Length + 1];
124.                     Array.Copy(block.Items, newItems, (int)block.Length);
125.                     newItems[block.Length] = value;
126.                     block.Length++;
127.                     block.Items = newItems;
128.                     return;
129.                 }
130.             }
131.             CacheEntryBlock<T> newBlock = new CacheEntryBlock<T>() { FirstIndex = index, Length = 1, Items = new T[] { value } };
132.             cacheBlocks.Insert(insertBeforeBlock, newBlock);
133.         }
134. 
135. 
136.         /// <summary>
137.         /// Updates the desired item range of the cache, discarding items that are not needed, and figuring out which items need to be requested. It will then kick off a fetch if required.
138.         /// </summary>
139.         /// <param name="ranges">New set of ranges the cache should hold</param>
140.         public void UpdateRanges(ItemIndexRange[] ranges)
141.         {
142.             //Normalize ranges to get a unique set of discontinuous ranges
143.             ranges = NormalizeRanges(ranges);
144. 
145.             // Fail fast if the ranges haven't changed
146.             if (!HasRangesChanged(ranges)) { return; }
147. 
148.             //To make the cache update easier, we'll create a new set of CacheEntryBlocks
149.             List<CacheEntryBlock<T>> newCacheBlocks = new List<CacheEntryBlock<T>>();
150.             foreach (ItemIndexRange range in ranges)
151.             {
152.                 CacheEntryBlock<T> newBlock = new CacheEntryBlock<T>() { FirstIndex = range.FirstIndex, Length = range.Length, Items = new T[range.Length] };
153.                 newCacheBlocks.Add(newBlock);
154.             }
155. 
156. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
157.             string s = "┌ " + debugName + ".UpdateRanges: ";
158.             foreach (ItemIndexRange range in ranges)
159.             {
160.                 s += range.FirstIndex + "->" + range.LastIndex + " ";
161.             }
162.             Debug.WriteLine(s);
163. #endif
164.             //Copy over data to the new cache blocks from the old ones where there is overlap
165.             int lastTransferred = 0;
166.             for (int i = 0; i < ranges.Length; i++)
167.             {
168.                 CacheEntryBlock<T> newBlock = newCacheBlocks[i];
169.                 ItemIndexRange range = ranges[i];
170.                 int j = lastTransferred;
171.                 while (j < this.cacheBlocks.Count && this.cacheBlocks[j].FirstIndex <= ranges[i].LastIndex)
172.                 {
173.                     ItemIndexRange overlap, oldEntryRange;
174.                     ItemIndexRange[] added, removed;
175.                     CacheEntryBlock<T> oldBlock = this.cacheBlocks[j];
176.                     oldEntryRange = new ItemIndexRange(oldBlock.FirstIndex, oldBlock.Length);
177.                     bool hasOverlap = oldEntryRange.DiffRanges(range, out overlap, out removed, out added);
178.                     if (hasOverlap)
179.                     {
180.                         Array.Copy(oldBlock.Items, overlap.FirstIndex - oldBlock.FirstIndex, newBlock.Items, overlap.FirstIndex - range.FirstIndex, (int)overlap.Length);
181. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
182.                         Debug.WriteLine("│ Transfering cache items " + overlap.FirstIndex + "->" + overlap.LastIndex);
183. #endif
184.                     }
185.                     j++;
186.                     if (ranges.Length > i + 1 && oldBlock.lastIndex < ranges[i + 1].FirstIndex) { lastTransferred = j; }
187.                 }
188.             }
189.             //swap over to the new cache
190.             this.cacheBlocks = newCacheBlocks;
191. 
192.             //figure out what items need to be fetched because we don't have them in the cache
193.             this.requests = new ItemIndexRangeList(ranges);
194.             ItemIndexRangeList newCachedResults = new ItemIndexRangeList();
195. 
196.             // Use the previous knowlege of what we have cached to form the new list
197.             foreach (ItemIndexRange range in ranges)
198.             {
199.                 foreach (ItemIndexRange cached in this.cachedResults)
200.                 {
201.                     ItemIndexRange overlap;
202.                     ItemIndexRange[] added, removed;
203.                     bool hasOverlap = cached.DiffRanges(range, out overlap, out removed, out added);
204.                     if (hasOverlap) { newCachedResults.Add(overlap); }
205.                 }
206.             }
207.             // remove the data we know we have cached from the results
208.             foreach (ItemIndexRange range in newCachedResults)
209.             {
210.                 this.requests.Subtract(range);
211.             }
212.             this.cachedResults = newCachedResults;
213. 
214.             startFetchData();
215. 
216. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
217.             s = "└ Pending requests: ";
218.             foreach (ItemIndexRange range in this.requests)
219.             {
220.                 s += range.FirstIndex + "->" + range.LastIndex + " ";
221.             }
222.             Debug.WriteLine(s);
223. #endif 
224.         }
225. 
226.         // Compares the new ranges against the previous ones to see if they have changed
227.         private bool HasRangesChanged(ItemIndexRange[] ranges)
228.         {
229.             if (ranges.Length != cacheBlocks.Count)
230.             {
231.                 return true;
232.             }
233.             for (int i = 0; i < ranges.Length; i++)
234.             {
235.                 ItemIndexRange r = ranges[i];
236.                 CacheEntryBlock<T> block = this.cacheBlocks[i];
237.                 if (r.FirstIndex != block.FirstIndex || r.LastIndex != block.lastIndex)
238.                 {
239.                     return true;
240.                 }
241.             }
242.             return false;
243.         }
244. 
245.         // Gets the first block of items that we don't have values for
246.         public ItemIndexRange GetFirstRequestBlock(int maxsize = 50)
247.         {
248.             if (this.requests.Count > 0)
249.             {
250.                 ItemIndexRange range = this.requests[0];
251.                 if (range.Length > 50) range = new ItemIndexRange(range.FirstIndex, 50);
252.                 return range;
253.             }
254.             return null;
255.         }
256. 
257. 
258.         // Throttling function for fetching data. Forces a wait of 20ms before making the request.
259.         // If another fetch is requested in that time, it will reset the timer, so we don't fetch data if the view is actively scrolling
260.         public void startFetchData()
261.         {
262.             // Verify if an active request is still needed
263.             if (this.requestInProgress != null)
264.             {
265.                 if (this.requests.Intersects(requestInProgress))
266.                 {
267.                     return;
268.                 }
269.                 else
270.                 {
271.                     //cancel the existing request
272. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
273.                     Debug.WriteLine("> " + debugName + " Cancelling request: " + requestInProgress.FirstIndex + "->" + requestInProgress.LastIndex);
274. #endif
275.                     cancelTokenSource.Cancel();
276.                 }
277.             }
278. 
279.             //Using a timer to delay fetching data by 20ms, if another range comes in that time, then the timer is reset.
280.             timer.Stop();
281.             timer.Start();
282.         }
283. 
284.         public delegate Task<T[]> fetchDataCallbackHandler(ItemIndexRange range, CancellationToken ct);
285. 
286.         // Called by the timer to make a request for data
287.         public async void fetchData()
288.         {
289.             //Stop the timer so we don't get fired again unless data is requested
290.             timer.Stop();
291.             if (this.requestInProgress != null)
292.             {
293.                 // Verify if an active request is still needed
294.                 if (this.requests.Intersects(requestInProgress))
295.                 {
296.                     return;
297.                 }
298.                 else
299.                 {
300.                     // Cancel the existing request
301. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
302.                     Debug.WriteLine(">" + debugName + " Cancelling request: " + requestInProgress.FirstIndex + "->" + requestInProgress.LastIndex);
303. #endif
304.                     cancelTokenSource.Cancel();
305.                 }
306.             }
307. 
308.             ItemIndexRange nextRequest = GetFirstRequestBlock(maxBatchFetchSize);
309.             if (nextRequest != null)
310.             {
311.                 cancelTokenSource = new CancellationTokenSource();
312.                 CancellationToken ct = cancelTokenSource.Token;
313.                 requestInProgress = nextRequest;
314.                 T[] data = null;
315.                 try
316.                 {
317. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
318.                     Debug.WriteLine(">" + debugName + " Fetching items " + nextRequest.FirstIndex + "->" + nextRequest.LastIndex);
319. #endif
320.                     // Use the callback to get the data, passing in a cancellation token
321.                     data = await fetchDataCallback(nextRequest, ct);
322. 
323.                     if (!ct.IsCancellationRequested)
324.                     {
325. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
326.                         Debug.WriteLine(">" + debugName + " Inserting items into cache at: " + nextRequest.FirstIndex + "->" + (nextRequest.FirstIndex + data.Length - 1));
327. #endif
328.                         for (int i = 0; i < data.Length; i++)
329.                         {
330.                             int cacheIndex = (int)(nextRequest.FirstIndex + i);
331. 
332.                             T oldItem = this[cacheIndex];
333.                             T newItem = data[i];
334. 
335.                             if (!newItem.Equals(oldItem))
336.                             {
337.                                 this[cacheIndex] = newItem;
338. 
339.                                 // Fire CacheChanged so that the datasource can fire its INCC event, and do other work based on the item having data
340.                                 if (CacheChanged != null)
341.                                 {
342.                                     CacheChanged(this, new CacheChangedEventArgs<T>() { oldItem = oldItem, newItem = newItem, itemIndex = cacheIndex });
343.                                 }
344.                             }
345.                         }
346.                         requests.Subtract(new ItemIndexRange(nextRequest.FirstIndex, (uint)data.Length));
347.                     }
348.                 }
349.                 // Try/Catch is needed as cancellation is via an exception
350.                 catch (OperationCanceledException) { }
351.                 finally
352.                 {
353.                     requestInProgress = null;
354.                     // Start another request if required
355.                     fetchData();
356.                 }
357.             }
358.         }
359. 
360. 
361.         /// <summary>
362.         /// Merges a set of ranges to form a new set of non-contiguous ranges
363.         /// </summary>
364.         /// <param name="ranges">The list of ranges to merge</param>
365.         /// <returns>A smaller set of merged ranges</returns>
366.         private ItemIndexRange[] NormalizeRanges(ItemIndexRange[] ranges)
367.         {
368.             List<ItemIndexRange> results = new List<ItemIndexRange>();
369.             foreach (ItemIndexRange range in ranges)
370.             {
371.                 bool handled = false;
372.                 for (int i = 0; i < results.Count; i++)
373.                 {
374.                     ItemIndexRange existing = results[i];
375.                     if (range.ContiguousOrOverlaps(existing))
376.                     {
377.                         results[i] = existing.Combine(range);
378.                         handled = true;
379.                         break;
380.                     }
381.                     else if (range.FirstIndex < existing.FirstIndex)
382.                     {
383.                         results.Insert(i, range);
384.                         handled = true;
385.                         break;
386.                     }
387.                 }
388.                 if (!handled) { results.Add(range); }
389.             }
390.             return results.ToArray();
391.         }
392. 
393. 
394.         // Sees if the value is in our cache if so it returns the index
395.         public int IndexOf(T value)
396.         {
397.             foreach (CacheEntryBlock<T> entry in cacheBlocks)
398.             {
399.                 int index = Array.IndexOf<T>(entry.Items, value);
400.                 if (index != -1) return index + entry.FirstIndex;
401.             }
402.             return -1;
403.         }
404. 
405.         // Type for the cache blocks
406.         class CacheEntryBlock<ITEMTYPE>
407.         {
408.             public int FirstIndex;
409.             public uint Length;
410.             public ITEMTYPE[] Items;
411. 
412.             public int lastIndex { get { return FirstIndex + (int)Length - 1; } }
413.         }
414.     }

36. 
37.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
38.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
39.             Current = this;
40.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
41.         }
42. 
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = scenarios;
47.             if (App.MainWindow!.Bounds.Width < NarrowWidthThreshold)
48.             {
49.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
50.             }
51.             else
52.             {
53.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
54.             }
55.         }
56. 
57.         /// <summary>
58.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
59.         /// sample scenario page.
60.         /// </summary>
61.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
62.         /// <param name="e"></param>
63.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
64.         {
65.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
66.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
67. 
68.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
69.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
70.             if (s != null)
71.             {
72.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
73.                 if (App.MainWindow!.Bounds.Width < NarrowWidthThreshold)
74.                 {
75.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
76.                     StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
77.                 }
78.             }
79.         }
80. 
81.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
82.         {
83.             get { return this.scenarios; }
84.         }
85. 
86.         /// <summary>
87.         /// Used to display messages to the user
88.         /// </summary>
89.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
90.         /// <param name="type"></param>
91.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
92.         {
93.             switch (type)
94.             {
95.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
96.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Colors.Green);
97.                     break;
98.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
99.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Colors.Red);
100.                     break;
101.             }
102.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
103. 
104.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
105.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
106.         }
107. 
108.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
109.         {
110.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
111.         }
112. 
113.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
114.         {
115.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
116.             StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
117.         }
118.     }
119.     public enum NotifyType
120.     {
121.         StatusMessage,
122.         ErrorMessage
123.     };

125.     public class ScenarioBindingConverter : IValueConverter
126.     {
127.         public object Convert(object value, Type targetType, object parameter, string language)
128.         {
129.             Scenario s = value as Scenario;
130.             return (MainPage.Current.Scenarios.IndexOf(s) + 1) + ") " + s.Title;
131.         }
132. 
133.         public object ConvertBack(object value, Type targetType, object parameter, string language)
134.         {
135.             return true;
136.         }

15.     //********************************************************************************************
16.     //*
17.     //* Note: This sample uses a custom compiler constant to enable tracing. If you add
18.     //* TRACE_DATASOURCE to the Conditional compilation symbols of the Build tab of the
