24.     /// <summary>
25.     /// A custom datasource over the file system that supports data virtualization
26.     /// </summary>
27.     class FileDataSource2 : INotifyCollectionChanged, System.Collections.IList, IItemsRangeInfo, ISelectionInfo
28.     {
29.         // Folder that we are browsing
30.         private StorageFolder _folder;
31.         // Query object that will tell us if the folder content changed
32.         private StorageFileQueryResult _queryResult;
33.         // Dispatcher so we can marshal calls back to the UI thread
34.         // TODO[migrate-006]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
35.         private DispatcherQueue _dispatcherQueue;
36. 
37.         // Cache for the file data that is currently being used
38.         private ItemCacheManager<FileItem> itemCache;
39. 
40.         // Total number of files available
41.         private int _count = 1;
42. 
43.         public event NotifyCollectionChangedEventHandler CollectionChanged;
44. 
45.         private FileDataSource2()
46.         {
47.             //Setup the dispatcher for the UI thread
48.             _dispatcherQueue = DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread();
49. 
50.             // The ItemCacheManager does most of the heavy lifting. We pass it a callback that it will use to actually fetch data, and the max size of a request
51.             this.itemCache = new ItemCacheManager<FileItem>(fetchDataCallback, 50);
52.             this.itemCache.CacheChanged += ItemCache_CacheChanged;
53. 
54.             // Create another ItemCacheManager for the keys for selection
55.             this.selectionCache = new ItemCacheManager<string>(fetchSelectionDataCallback, 50, "selectionCache");
56.         }
57. 
58.         // Handles a change notification for the list of files from the OS
59.         public async void ResetCollection()
60.         {
61.             // Update the selection cache to match the filesystem after the updates
62.             await RemapSelection();
63. 
64.             // Unhook the old change notification
65.             if (itemCache != null)
66.             {
67.                 this.itemCache.CacheChanged -= ItemCache_CacheChanged;
68.             }
69. 
70.             // Create a new instance of the cache manager
71.             this.itemCache = new ItemCacheManager<FileItem>(fetchDataCallback, 50);
72.             this.itemCache.CacheChanged += ItemCache_CacheChanged;
73.             if (CollectionChanged != null)
74.             {
75.                 CollectionChanged(this, new NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs(NotifyCollectionChangedAction.Reset));
76.             }
77.         }
78. 
79. 
80.         //
81.         // Implementation of ISelectionInfo
82.         //
83. 
84.         // Storage for the keys of the selected items
85.         private ItemCacheManager<string> selectionCache;
86.         // List of ranges that form the selection
87.         private ItemIndexRangeList selection = new ItemIndexRangeList();
88. 
89.         // Called when an item (or items) are selected
90.         public void SelectRange(ItemIndexRange range)
91.         {
92.             selection.Add(range);
93.             selectionCache.UpdateRanges(selection.ToArray());
94.         }
95. 
96.         // Called when an item (or items) are deselected
97.         public void DeselectRange(ItemIndexRange range)
98.         {
99.             selection.Subtract(range);
100.             selectionCache.UpdateRanges(selection.ToArray());
101.         }
102. 
103.         //  Called to determine if an item is selected 
104.         public bool IsSelected(int index)
105.         {
106.             foreach (ItemIndexRange range in selection)
107.             {
108.                 if (index >= range.FirstIndex && index <= range.LastIndex) return true;
109.             }
110.             return false;
111.         }
112. 
113.         // Called to get the selected ranges 
114.         public IReadOnlyList<ItemIndexRange> GetSelectedRanges()
115.         {
116.             return selection.ToList();
117.         }
118. 
119.         // Callback from the selection cache manager
120.         // Retrieves the keys for selected items
121.         private async Task<string[]> fetchSelectionDataCallback(ItemIndexRange batch, CancellationToken ct)
122.         {
123. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
124.             Debug.WriteLine("# SelectionDataCallback: " + batch.FirstIndex + "->" + batch.LastIndex);
125. #endif
126.             // See if we already have the item in the data cache, if so get the key from there so we don't need to go to the filesystem
127.             var file = itemCache[batch.FirstIndex];
128.             if (file != null)
129.             {
130.                 return new string[] { file.Key };
131.             }
132. 
133.             // Go get the keys from the file system if necessary
134.             IReadOnlyList<StorageFile> results = await _queryResult.GetFilesAsync((uint)batch.FirstIndex, batch.Length).AsTask(ct);
135.             List<string> keys = new List<string>();
136.             if (results != null)
137.             {
138.                 for (int i = 0; i < results.Count; i++)
139.                 {
140.                     ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
141.                     keys.Add(results[i].FolderRelativeId);
142.                 }
143.             }
144.             return keys.ToArray();
145.         }
146. 
147.         // Called after a reset to figure out the new indexes for the selected items
148.         private async Task RemapSelection()
149.         {
150.             ItemIndexRangeList oldSelection = selection;
151.             ItemCacheManager<string> oldSelectionCache = selectionCache;
152.             ItemIndexRangeList newSelection = new ItemIndexRangeList();
153.             ItemCacheManager<string> newSelectionCache = new ItemCacheManager<string>(fetchSelectionDataCallback, 50, "newSelectionCache");
154. 
155.             foreach (ItemIndexRange r in oldSelection)
156.             {
157.                 IReadOnlyList<StorageFile> results = null;
158.                 int lastResultOffset = 0, lastResultsItemIndex = 0;
159.                 for (int i = 0; i < r.Length; i++)
160.                 {
161.                     int origIndex = r.FirstIndex + i;
162.                     string fileid = oldSelectionCache[origIndex];
163.                     bool matched = false;
164. 
165.                     // Optimization to be able to work in batches. Once we get a batch of files from the filesystem we use that to hunt
166.                     // for matches rather than having to go ask for the index of each file
167.                     if (results != null)
168.                     {
169.                         for (int j = lastResultOffset + 1; j < results.Count; j++)
170.                         {
171.                             if (results[j].FolderRelativeId == fileid)
172.                             {
173.                                 lastResultOffset = j;
174.                                 int itemIndex = lastResultsItemIndex + j;
175.                                 newSelection.Add((uint)itemIndex, 1);
176.                                 newSelectionCache[itemIndex] = oldSelectionCache[origIndex];
177.                                 matched = true;
178.                                 break;
179.                             }
180.                         }
181.                     }
182.                     if (!matched)
183.                     {
184.                         // Get a starting point for the index of the file
185.                         lastResultsItemIndex = (int)(await _queryResult.FindStartIndexAsync(fileid.Substring(fileid.LastIndexOf('\\') + 1)));
186.                         lastResultOffset = 0;
187.                         // Get the files at that point and see if the keys actually match
188.                         results = await _queryResult.GetFilesAsync((uint)lastResultsItemIndex, 50);
189.                         if (results[lastResultOffset].FolderRelativeId == fileid)
190.                         {
191.                             newSelection.Add((uint)lastResultsItemIndex, 1);
192.                             newSelectionCache[lastResultsItemIndex] = oldSelectionCache[origIndex];
193.                         }
194.                         else
195.                         {
196.                             // We can't find the item, so its no longer part of the selection
197.                         }
198.                     }
199.                 }
200.             }
201.             // Update the fields for the new selection
202.             selection = newSelection;
203.             selectionCache = newSelectionCache;
204.         }
205. 
206.         #region Filesystem specific implementation details
207. 
208.         // Factory method to create the datasource
209.         // Requires async work which is why it needs a factory rather than being part of the constructor
210.         public static async Task<FileDataSource2> GetDataSoure(string path)
211.         {
212.             FileDataSource2 ds = new FileDataSource2();
213.             StorageFolder f = await StorageFolder.GetFolderFromPathAsync(path);
214.             await ds.SetFolder(f);
215.             return ds;
216.         }
217. 
218.         // Set functionality for the folder
219.         public async Task SetFolder(StorageFolder folder)
220.         {
221.             //unhook the old contents changed event if applicable
222.             if (_queryResult != null)
223.             {
224.                 _queryResult.ContentsChanged -= QueryResult_ContentsChanged;
225.             }
226.             // Initialize the query and register for changes
227.             _folder = folder;
228.             QueryOptions options = new QueryOptions();
229.             // options.IndexerOption = IndexerOption.DoNotUseIndexer;
230.             _queryResult = _folder.CreateFileQueryWithOptions(options);
231.             _queryResult.ContentsChanged += QueryResult_ContentsChanged;
232.             await UpdateCount();
233.         }
234. 
235.         // Handler for when the filesystem notifies us of a change to the file list
236.         private void QueryResult_ContentsChanged(IStorageQueryResultBase sender, object args)
237.         {
238.             // This callback can occur on a different thread so we need to marshal it back to the UI thread
239.             if (!_dispatcherQueue.HasThreadAccess)
240.             {
241.                 _dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(ResetCollection);
242.             }
243.             else
244.             {
245.                 ResetCollection();
246.             }
247.         }
248.         async Task UpdateCount()
249.         {
250.             _count = (int)await _queryResult.GetItemCountAsync();
251.             if (CollectionChanged != null)
252.             {
253.                 CollectionChanged(this, new NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs(NotifyCollectionChangedAction.Reset));
254.             }
255.         }
256. 
257.         //Event fired when items are inserted in the cache
258.         //Used to fire our collection changed event
259.         private void ItemCache_CacheChanged(object sender, CacheChangedEventArgs<FileItem> args)
260.         {
261.             if (CollectionChanged != null)
262.             {
263.                 CollectionChanged(this, new NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs(NotifyCollectionChangedAction.Replace, args.oldItem, args.newItem, args.itemIndex));
264.             }
265.         }
266.         #endregion
267. 
268.         #region IList Implementation
269. 
270.         public bool Contains(object value)
271.         {
272.             return IndexOf(value) != -1;
273.         }
274. 
275.         public int IndexOf(object value)
276.         {
277.             return (value != null) ? itemCache.IndexOf((FileItem)value) : -1;
278.         }
279. 
280.         public object this[int index]
281.         {
282.             get
283.             {
284.                 // The cache will return null if it doesn't have the item. Once the item is fetched it will fire a changed event so that we can inform the list control
285.                 return itemCache[index];
286.             }
287.             set
288.             {
289.                 throw new NotImplementedException();
290.             }
291.         }
292.         public int Count
293.         {
294.             get { return _count; }
295.         }
296. 
297.         #endregion
298. 
299.         #region IItemsRangeInfo Implementation
300.         //Required for the IItemsRangeInfo interface
301.         public void Dispose()
302.         {
303.             itemCache = null;
304.         }
305. 
306.         /// <summary>
307.         /// Primary method for IItemsRangeInfo interface
308.         /// Is called when the list control's view is changed
309.         /// </summary>
310.         /// <param name="visibleRange">The range of items that are actually visible</param>
311.         /// <param name="trackedItems">Additional set of ranges that the list is using, for example the buffer regions and focussed element</param>
312.         public void RangesChanged(ItemIndexRange visibleRange, IReadOnlyList<ItemIndexRange> trackedItems)
313.         {
314. #if TRACE_DATASOURCE
315.             string s = string.Format("* RangesChanged fired: Visible {0}->{1}", visibleRange.FirstIndex, visibleRange.LastIndex);
316.             foreach (ItemIndexRange r in trackedItems) { s += string.Format(" {0}->{1}", r.FirstIndex, r.LastIndex); }
317.             Debug.WriteLine(s);
318. #endif
319.             // We know that the visible range is included in the broader range so don't need to hand it to the UpdateRanges call
320.             // Update the cache of items based on the new set of ranges. It will callback for additional data if required
321.             itemCache.UpdateRanges(trackedItems.ToArray());
322.         }
323. 
324. 
325.         // Callback from itemcache that it needs items to be retrieved
326.         private async Task<FileItem[]> fetchDataCallback(ItemIndexRange batch, CancellationToken ct)
327.         {
328.             // Fetch file objects from filesystem
329.             IReadOnlyList<StorageFile> results = await _queryResult.GetFilesAsync((uint)batch.FirstIndex, Math.Max(batch.Length, 20)).AsTask(ct);
330.             List<FileItem> files = new List<FileItem>();
331.             if (results != null)
332.             {
333.                 for (int i = 0; i < results.Count; i++)
334.                 {
335.                     ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
336.                     // Create our FileItem object with the file data and thumbnail 
337.                     FileItem newItem = await FileItem.fromStorageFile(results[i], ct);
338.                     files.Add(newItem);
339.                 }
340.             }
341.             return files.ToArray();
342.         }

179.         // Callback from itemcache that it needs items to be retrieved
180.         // Using this callback model abstracts the details of this specific datasource from the cache implementation
181.         private async Task<FileItem[]> fetchDataCallback(ItemIndexRange batch, CancellationToken ct)
182.         {
183.             // Fetch file objects from filesystem
184.             IReadOnlyList<StorageFile> results = await _queryResult.GetFilesAsync((uint)batch.FirstIndex, Math.Max(batch.Length, 20)).AsTask(ct);
185.             List<FileItem> files = new List<FileItem>();
186.             if (results != null)
187.             {
188.                 for (int i = 0; i < results.Count; i++)
189.                 {
190.                     // Check if request has been cancelled, if so abort getting additional data
191.                     ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
192.                     // Create our FileItem object with the file data and thumbnail 
193.                     FileItem newItem = await FileItem.fromStorageFile(results[i], ct);
194.                     files.Add(newItem);
195.                 }
196.             }
197.             return files.ToArray();
198.         }
199.

205.             {
206.                 CollectionChanged(this, new NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs(NotifyCollectionChangedAction.Replace, args.oldItem, args.newItem, args.itemIndex));

16. {
17.     // Extension methods for ItemIndexRange
18.     public static class ItemIndexRangeExtensions
19.     {
20.         public static bool Equals(this ItemIndexRange This, ItemIndexRange range)
21.         {
22.             return (This.FirstIndex == range.FirstIndex && This.Length == range.Length);
23.         }
24. 
25.         public static bool ContiguousOrOverlaps(this ItemIndexRange This, ItemIndexRange range)
26.         {
27.             // This is left
28.             if(This.FirstIndex < range.FirstIndex)
29.             {
30.                 return (range.FirstIndex <= This.LastIndex + 1);
31.             }
32.             // This is right
33.             else if (This.FirstIndex > range.FirstIndex)
34.             {
35.                 return (This.FirstIndex <= range.LastIndex + 1);
36.             }
37. 
38.             // Aligned
39.             return true;
40.         }
41. 
42.         public static bool Intersects(this ItemIndexRange This, ItemIndexRange range)
43.         {
44.             return (range.FirstIndex >= This.FirstIndex && range.FirstIndex <= This.LastIndex) || (range.LastIndex >= This.FirstIndex && range.LastIndex <= This.LastIndex);
45.         }
46. 
47.         public static bool Intersects(this ItemIndexRange This, int FirstIndex, uint Length)
48.         {
49.             int LastIndex = FirstIndex + (int)Length - 1;
50.             return (FirstIndex >= This.FirstIndex && FirstIndex <= This.LastIndex) || (LastIndex >= This.FirstIndex && LastIndex <= This.LastIndex);
51.         }
52. 
53.         public static ItemIndexRange Combine(this ItemIndexRange This, ItemIndexRange range)
54.         {
55.             int start = Math.Min(This.FirstIndex, range.FirstIndex);
56.             int end = Math.Max(This.LastIndex, range.LastIndex);
57. 
58.             return new ItemIndexRange(start, 1 + (uint)Math.Abs(end - start));
59.         }
60. 
61.         public static bool DiffRanges(this ItemIndexRange RangeA, ItemIndexRange RangeB, out ItemIndexRange InBothAandB, out ItemIndexRange[] OnlyInRangeA, out ItemIndexRange[] OnlyInRangeB)
62.         {
63.             List<ItemIndexRange> exA = new List<ItemIndexRange>();
64.             List<ItemIndexRange> exB = new List<ItemIndexRange>();
65.             int i, j;
66.             i = Math.Max(RangeA.FirstIndex, RangeB.FirstIndex);
67.             j = Math.Min(RangeA.LastIndex, RangeB.LastIndex);
68. 
69.             if (i <= j)
70.             {
71.                 // Ranges intersect
72.                 InBothAandB = new ItemIndexRange(i, (uint)(1 + j - i));
73.                 if (RangeA.FirstIndex < i) exA.Add(new ItemIndexRange(RangeA.FirstIndex, (uint)(i - RangeA.FirstIndex)));
74.                 if (RangeA.LastIndex > j) exA.Add(new ItemIndexRange(j + 1, (uint)(RangeA.LastIndex - j)));
75.                 if (RangeB.FirstIndex < i) exB.Add(new ItemIndexRange(RangeB.FirstIndex, (uint)(i - RangeB.FirstIndex)));
76.                 if (RangeB.LastIndex > j) exB.Add(new ItemIndexRange(j + 1, (uint)(RangeB.LastIndex - j)));
77.                 OnlyInRangeA = exA.ToArray();
78.                 OnlyInRangeB = exB.ToArray();
79.                 return true;
80.             }
81.             else
82.             {
83.                 InBothAandB = default(ItemIndexRange);
84.                 OnlyInRangeA = new ItemIndexRange[] { RangeA };
85.                 OnlyInRangeB = new ItemIndexRange[] { RangeB };
86.                 return false;
87.             }
88.         }
89. 
90.         public static ItemIndexRange Overlap(this ItemIndexRange RangeA, ItemIndexRange RangeB)
91.         {
92.             int i, j;
93.             i = Math.Max(RangeA.FirstIndex, RangeB.FirstIndex);
94.             j = Math.Min(RangeA.LastIndex, RangeB.LastIndex);
95. 
96.             if (i <= j)
97.             {
98.                 // Ranges intersect
99.                 return new ItemIndexRange(i, (uint)(1 + j - i));
100.             }
101.             else
102.             {
103.                 return null;
104.             }
105.         }
106.

17. {
18.     /// <summary>
19.     /// Represents a file and its properties
20.     /// </summary>
21.     class FileItem
22.     {
23.         public string Filename { get; set; }
24.         public int Size { get; set; }
25.         public BitmapImage ImageData { get; set; }
26. 
27.         public string Key { get; private set; }
28. 
29.         // Needed to ensure only one request is in progress at once
30.         private static SemaphoreSlim gettingFileProperties = new SemaphoreSlim(1);
31. 
32.         // Fetches all the data for the specified file
33.         public async static Task<FileItem> fromStorageFile(StorageFile f, CancellationToken ct)
34.         {
35.             FileItem item = new FileItem();
36.             item.Filename = f.DisplayName;
37.             
38.             // Block to make sure we only have one request outstanding
39.             await gettingFileProperties.WaitAsync();
40. 
41.             BasicProperties bp = null;
42.             try
43.             {
44.                 bp = await f.GetBasicPropertiesAsync().AsTask(ct);
45.             }
46.             catch (Exception) { }
47.             finally
48.             {
49.                 gettingFileProperties.Release();
50.             }
51. 
52.             ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
53. 
54.             item.Size = (int)bp.Size;
55.             item.Key = f.FolderRelativeId;
56. 
57.             StorageItemThumbnail thumb = await f.GetThumbnailAsync(ThumbnailMode.SingleItem).AsTask(ct);
58.             ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
59.             BitmapImage img = new BitmapImage();
60.             await img.SetSourceAsync(thumb).AsTask(ct);
61. 
62.             item.ImageData = img;
63.             return item;
64.         }
65.     }
66. }

<a id="startup-crashes"></a>
## Diagnosing Startup Crashes

A packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash the instant it launches — `winapp run` returns no usable window and the process is gone. The build log and the analyzer cannot see this; only a real launch does. The **wrong** way to chase it is to sprinkle `File.WriteAllText` traces through `Program.cs` / `App.xaml.cs` and re-run repeatedly — that burns time and usually misses the real frame (the crash often happens in native activation, before your managed `Main` even runs).

**Capture the real exception first.** Either run the launch-and-diagnose helper:

```powershell
& "<skill-root>/scripts/Test-AppLaunch.ps1" -Target "<winui3-project-root>"
```

…or read the Windows **Application** event log directly for the two events the crash writes:

```powershell
Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 40 |
  Where-Object { $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) } |
  ForEach-Object { "`n=== Event $($_.Id) @ $($_.TimeCreated) ==="; $_.Message }
```

- **Event 1000** — faulting module + native **Exception code** (e.g. `0x8001010E`).
- **Event 1026** — *.NET Runtime*: the managed **exception type, message, and stack**. This is the one that names the actual throwing frame. Read it before changing any code.

**Known native codes and what they usually mean** (the code names the error *class*; the real fix is whatever the captured stack points at):

| Exception code | Meaning | Usual migration cause → where to look |
|---|---|---|
| `0x80004003` | `E_POINTER` | Static-window **init-order race** — a `Page` read `App.MainWindow` (or another static window reference) before `OnLaunched` assigned it. Keep `MainWindow`'s constructor inert and navigate after `Activate`. See [Initialization order](#windowing). |
| `0x8001010E` | `RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD` | A **thread/apartment-affined object** was accessed during startup — commonly a view- or `CoreWindow`-affined UWP API touched from a `static` initializer, a type constructor, or off the UI thread. Construct/access it on the UI thread *after* `Activate`. If the API has no WinUI 3 desktop equivalent, defer it. |
| `0xE0434352` | Managed CLR exception | Read the **.NET exception type** in event 1026. `TypeLoadException` / `FileNotFoundException` almost always means a missing or version-incompatible package reference, not your code. |
| `0xC000027B` | Native stowed exception | Often a legacy projection/activation incompatibility for an API used at startup. If the API/contract is unsupported on the current OS, defer it. |

> Do **not** assume the entry point is the problem. A custom `Program.Main` for WinUI 3 **correctly** carries `[STAThread]` + `ComWrappersSupport.InitializeComWrappers()` + the `DispatcherQueueSynchronizationContext` setup — this matches the SDK's auto-generated `Main`. `[STAThread]` is **required**, not a bug. If you have a hand-written entry point and don't need single-instancing/redirection, the simplest path is to delete it and let the SDK generate `Main`.

The Step 4 validator runs this same check (`Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 7) and **fails** when the app registers but dies at startup, surfacing the captured signature in `.validator-diagnostics.txt`.

26.     /// A custom datasource over the file system that supports data virtualization
27.     /// </summary>
28.     class FileDataSource : INotifyCollectionChanged, System.Collections.IList, IItemsRangeInfo
29.     {
30.         // Folder that we are browsing
31.         private StorageFolder _folder;
32.         // Query object that will tell us if the folder content changed
33.         private StorageFileQueryResult _queryResult;
34.         // Dispatcher so we can marshal calls back to the UI thread
35.         private DispatcherQueue _dispatcherQueue;
36. 
37.         // Cache for the file data that is currently being used
38.         private ItemCacheManager<FileItem> itemCache;
39. 
40.         // Total number of files available
41.         private int _count = 1;
42. 
43.         public event NotifyCollectionChangedEventHandler CollectionChanged;
44. 
45.         private FileDataSource()
46.         {
47.             //Setup the dispatcher for the UI thread
48.             _dispatcherQueue = DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread();
49. 
50.             // The ItemCacheManager does most of the heavy lifting. We pass it a callback that it will use to actually fetch data, and the max size of a request
51.             this.itemCache = new ItemCacheManager<FileItem>(fetchDataCallback, 50);
52.             this.itemCache.CacheChanged += ItemCache_CacheChanged;
53.         }
54. 
55.         // Factory method to create the datasource
56.         // Requires async work which is why it needs a factory rather than being part of the constructor
57.         public static async Task<FileDataSource> GetDataSoure(string path)
58.         {
59.             FileDataSource ds = new FileDataSource();
60.             StorageFolder f = await StorageFolder.GetFolderFromPathAsync(path);
61.             await ds.SetFolder(f);
62.             return ds;
63.         }
64. 
65.         // Set functionality for the folder
66.         public async Task SetFolder(StorageFolder folder)
67.         {
68.             //unhook the old contents changed event if applicable
69.             if (_queryResult != null)
70.             {
71.                 _queryResult.ContentsChanged -= QueryResult_ContentsChanged;
72.             }
73.             // Initialize the query and register for changes
74.             _folder = folder;
75.             QueryOptions options = new QueryOptions();
76.             // options.IndexerOption = IndexerOption.DoNotUseIndexer;
77.             _queryResult = _folder.CreateFileQueryWithOptions(options);
78.             _queryResult.ContentsChanged += QueryResult_ContentsChanged;
79.             await UpdateCount();
80.         }
81. 
82.         // Handler for when the filesystem notifies us of a change to the file list
83.         private void QueryResult_ContentsChanged(IStorageQueryResultBase sender, object args)
84.         {
85.             // This callback can occur on a different thread so we need to marshal it back to the UI thread
86.             if (!_dispatcherQueue.HasThreadAccess)
87.             {
88.                 _dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(ResetCollection);
89.             }
90.             else
91.             {
92.                 ResetCollection();
93.             }
94.         }
95. 
96.         // Handles a change notification for the list of files from the OS
97.         private void ResetCollection()
98.         {
99.             // Unhook the old change notification
100.             if (itemCache != null)
101.             {
102.                 this.itemCache.CacheChanged -= ItemCache_CacheChanged;
103.             }
104. 
105.             // Create a new instance of the cache manager
106.             this.itemCache = new ItemCacheManager<FileItem>(fetchDataCallback, 50);
107.             this.itemCache.CacheChanged += ItemCache_CacheChanged;
108.             if (CollectionChanged != null)
109.             {
110.                 CollectionChanged(this, new NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs(NotifyCollectionChangedAction.Reset));
111.             }
112.         }
113. 
114.         async Task UpdateCount()

116.             _count = (int)await _queryResult.GetItemCountAsync();
117.             if (CollectionChanged != null)
118.             {
119.                 CollectionChanged(this, new NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs(NotifyCollectionChangedAction.Reset));
